IT IS THE YEAR AD 23351.The setting is New Eden: a cluster of stars in a distant galaxy whoseinhabitants have not seen Earth since the wormhole collapsed over 16,000 years ago. Afterthousands of dark years in isolation, great empires have risen from the ashes, bent on dominance.This is the violent playground of a new breed of human: immortals who have defeated death throughtechnologyand now threatentoupsetthebalanceof power.EVE:Source provides an in-depth view into the gritty world of New Eden, the setting of CCP's award-winning games EVEOnline and DUST 514.Loaded with original artwork, short stories, essays,andvignettes, it aims to explain this dark universe in unprecedented detail, laying bare its interstellarnetwork of star gates,planets, cities,nations, criminals, cults,and curiosities.DARKDARKHORSE.COMHORSEBOOKSEVE.COM

INTRODUCTIONNew Eden. The violent playground of the immortals, over twenty thousand years into the future. Anabyss of darkness punctuated by the plasma of burning suns and celestial bodies of infinite diversity.New Eden is the place where our true potential can be released. As capsuleers and mercenaries,players of EVE Online and DUST 514 haye decided the fate of nations, built, destroyed, plundered-and,above all, triumphed. For over a decade, the EvE universe has been home to millions of immortals,roaming the heavens,all driven by different motives,forging their own destinies, clashing violently astheymeet in thedarknessof space.To us, it is home. From the heavily guarded, trash-strewn streets of Arcurio, the capital of theCaldari State, to the spectacular.incense-filled halls of the Amarr Empire,through the bustling crowdsin Matar's main marketplace, and on to the shiny. neon-lit boulevards of Caille in the heart of theGallente Federation ... this is where our capsuleers originate. This is where they were born.At last, we have a book that aims to capture the breadth of this vast universe and give you glimpsesinto its people and institutions, many of which have been veiled by the dark clouds that shelter thethousands of planets capsuleers pass on their quest to command the skies.New Eden is a universe in motion. Every molecule, every celestial body,every person is goingsomewhere. Nothing is at rest. Nothing can be taken for granted. The equilibrium of power, craftedby the old empires, is eroding as the capsuleer, the mercenary, and the Valkyrie pilot assert theirdominance. This book aims to capture a moment in time, the world as it is in the year AD 23351(or YC 115,according to the new calendar).How it will evolve remains to be seen.We hope you willsharethejourneywith us.
-TorfiFransOlafssonCreativeDirector,EVEIP

ORIGINSI have always found it remarkable that despite the vastness and breadth of this universe, we areall fundamentally so much the same-capable of such deep and genuine love for our nearest, andsuch astounding cruelty to those on the other side of whatever divide we choose to carve out nextto ourselves. We make art of such beauty that it blinds the eye, and wage wars of such ugliness thattheyblind the soul.Every place thus far inhabited by our species has been fractured by veins of discord, ultimatelycrumbling beneath the weight of our hatred. And yet, through a combination of blind providence andtechnological progress, we continually find new havens to sustain us. New Eden is mankind's latestsuch garden, one more chance to light the dark of space with motes of hopeful civilization. Would thatwecoulddream itwon't beourlast.
-Dr.DamellaMacaperTheSevenEventsoftheApocalypse

DARKHORIZONSWith a subject as significant and controversial as militarycloning. a certain degree of rumor and speculation isunavoidable. Though such talk covers a broad spectrum ofplausibility, most of it is fairly obvious hearsay and thus easilydismissed by a discerning listener. One rumor, however, hasrecently taken flight in the wake of a series of strange eventsthat appear to lend it some authority.For the last few months, capsuleer trainees all overNew Eden have been dropping out of their programs inever-increasing numbers and disappearing off the radarcompletely. often stealing highly specialized equipmentin the process. While the phenomenon of last-secondcold feet is a known factor in capsuleer training. thepervasiveness and uniformity of these larcenous vanishingacts is a growing cause for concern among the four empireseducational institutions.Though not an insignificant problem, this in and of itselfdid not cause a particular stir until a Caldari investigativejournalist uncovered evidence that one of the missingcapsuleer trainees had been identified as the pilot ofan experimental fighter shot down by a Caldari Navyinterceptor squad on scouting duty in the Tribute region.Upon inspection of his corpse, it was discovered that thepilot in question appeared to have had significant neuralmodifications made, the purpose and methodology ofwhich left scientists scratching their heads in bafflement.Furthermore,the brain showed an unnatural smoothnessin its ridges, a telltale sign that its electrochemical chargeshad been scanned. Whether this was done as a securitymeasure or for the purposes of an actual mind transfer hasnot been determined.The week after, a very similar incident took place inMinmatar territory. with more soon following all over NewEden. Amid growing concern for their capsuleer hopefulstraining facilities tightened their security measures, but tono avail:the siphoning of valuable talent continued unabated.When the investigative journalist revealed evidence thatindicated the first pilot's vessel was of Gurista design, shewas determined to find the truth of the matter. Undercover.she set out for a Guristas outpost where shipbuilding andengineering were rumored to take top priority. She hasnot been heard from since. Still today. rumors persist thatsomeone, somewhere, in the shadow of a blood moon, isworking on a breakthrough that will shake up the world ofNew Eden yetagain

A LOOK TOWARD THE FUTURE196

It has now been overa decade since the dawn of the immortalcapsuleer. In that short period of time, the universe of NewEden has transformed more than it did in the precedingcenturies. Established empires-some built over millenniawith blood, sweat, and tears-find their foundations shakenby what they see as self-serving individualists,void of moralsand lacking in respect for the old nations' ancient legacies.As in most conflict, those that suffer the most are not theleaders or the bureaucrats,but the unempowered people whohave come to depend on the old empires for some semblanceof stability and order in their simple lives, the strugglingtrillions that endure hardship and tyranny as an alternative toanarchy and the uncertainty that comes with absolute freedom.To the empire-building capsuleers soaring through space andthe mercenaries searing the ground on countless planets thesepeople are little more than statistics-scared faces inside cargocontainers, resources that can be shifted around, expensivegoods with a tendency to perish.Still, this is mankind's finest hour, the peak of civilizationand technological advancement. The potential of the humanintellect and willpower has been allowed to blossomunhindered, spawning magnificent innovations, bending thevery fabric of the universe to humanity's will.Despite all this technological progress, despite thememories of many terrible wars, and despite man's bestintentions of building everlasting peace, raw human natureappears to withstand evolution, continually pulling us back tothe primal instincts of greed, rage, and subjugation, all in thename of self-preservation.It is hard to predict what will become of the trillions ofhumans living in New Eden. What new alliances will thissocioeconomic transformation bring? How will the criminalorganizations scattered around the lawless regions ofthis universe adapt to the shifting balance of power? Willthe immortals continue their dominance unhindered, orare there forces on the horizon that we don't recognize atthis point in time that will enter the fray and counter theirgrowing power?
New Eden is a sprawling patchwork of star gates, jumpbridges, and unstable wormholes. Every minute, countlesscapsuleers rely on science far beyond their comprehension asthey are transported light years in mere seconds, en route toglory, death, or desolation. As large as the labyrinth of NewEden's traffic network is, aspiring capsuleers soon discoverthat it is not growing. New star gates, capable of taking usbeyond the cluster of known solar systems, cannot be builtModern science prohibits it. But what if those cosmologicalchallenges were overcome? What effect would a new era ofexploration and expansion have on the people of New Eden?
Who would gain? Who would suffer? And what wonders liebeyond the cosmic horizon?
We hope that in the coming decade, we will glimpse theanswers to some of these questions, as the immortals rise andbring us into a new age of mankind.197
